# Extension (Windows 8 package schema, in type: CT_PackageExtensions) - Windows UWP applications | Microsoft Learn

Declares an extensibility point for the package.

## Element hierarchy

- [&lt;Package&gt;](element-package)
    - - [&lt;Extensions&gt;](element-extensions)
    - **&lt;Extension&gt;**

## Syntax

```syntax
<Extension Category = "windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer" | "windows.activatableClass.outOfProcessServer" | "windows.activatableClass.proxyStub" | "windows.gameExplorer" | "windows.certificates" >

  <!-- Child elements -->
  ( InProcessServer
  | OutOfProcessServer
  | ProxyStub
  | GameExplorer
  | Certificates
  )

</Extension>
```

## Attributes and Elements

### Attributes

| Attribute | Description | Data type | Required | Default value |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Category** | The type of package extensibility point. | This attribute can have one of the following values:<br><br>- windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer<br>- windows.activatableClass.outOfProcessServer<br>- windows.activatableClass.proxyStub<br>- windows.gameExplorer<br>- windows.certificates | Yes |  |

### Child Elements

| Child Element | Description |
| --- | --- |
| [Certificates](element-certificates) | Declares a package extensibility point of type **windows.certificates**. The app requires one or more certificates from the specified certificate stores. |
| [GameExplorer](element-gameexplorer) | Declares a package extensibility point of type **windows.gameExplorer**. |
| [InProcessServer](element-inprocessserver) | Declares a package extensibility point of type **windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer**. The app uses a dynamic link library (DLL) that exposes one or more activatable classes. |
| [OutOfProcessServer](element-outofprocessserver) | Declares a package extension point of type **windows.activatableClass.outOfProcessServer**. The app uses an executable (EXE) that exposes one or more activatable classes. |
| [ProxyStub](element-proxystub) | Declares a package extensibility point of type **windows.activatableClass.proxyStub**. A proxy can be composed of one or more interfaces. |

### Parent Elements

| Parent Element | Description |
| --- | --- |
| [Extensions (type: CT_PackageExtensions)](element-extensions) | Defines one or more extensibility points for the package. |

## Related elements

The following elements have the same name as this one, but different content or attributes:

- **[Extension (in type: CT

<note>Content truncated. Call the fetch tool with a start_index of 5000 to get more content.</note>
## Summary

The root cause is a **fundamental API/model mismatch between UWP in-proc background tasks and WinUI 3 (Windows App SDK) background tasks**, not just a missing manifest stanza. In UWP, `windows.backgroundTasks` `EntryPoint` can point directly at your `IBackgroundTask`-implementing class name (`Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask`). In a WinUI 3 / Windows App SDK packaged desktop app, background tasks execute via a **full-trust COM component activated through `Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.BackgroundTaskBuilder`**, and the manifest `EntryPoint` must reference the Windows App SDK shim class, with your own class exposed as an activatable COM class via a `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer` extension. This is exactly what the validator error is flagging: `EntryPoint="Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask"` has no matching `ActivatableClassId` in a `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer` extension.

## Authoritative references (verified by fetch)

1. **Migration strategy (primary fix source)** — learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/background-task-migration-strategy — "In-proc background tasks" section: *"This won't be possible in a WinUI application as the OnBackgroundActivated callbacks aren't available. The application needs to move the background task implementations to full trust COM tasks... and define the COM server in the package manifest."* It gives the exact manifest snippet:
```xml
<Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.Task">
    <BackgroundTasks>
        <Task Type="general"/>
    </BackgroundTasks>
</Extension>

<!-- For C# apps, an ActivatableClass registration must also be added -->
<Extension Category="windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer">
    <InProcessServer>
        <Path>Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.dll</Path>
        <ActivatableClass ActivatableClassId="Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.Task" ThreadingModel="both"/>
    </InProcessServer>
</Extension>
```

2. **"Using background tasks in Windows apps"** — learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/applifecycle/background-tasks — confirms: *"C# apps must specify the EntryPoint attribute value 'Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.Task'. For C++ apps, this gets added automatically by setting `WindowsAppSDKBackgroundTask` to true in the project file."* Also shows the `com:Extension Category="windows.comServer"` block needed for out-of-proc/COM-registered activation and requires your task class to carry `[ComVisible(true)]`, `[Guid("...")]`, `[ComSourceInterfaces(typeof(IBackgroundTask))]`, and to be registered via `BackgroundTaskBuilder.SetTaskEntryPointClsid(typeof(BackgroundTask).GUID)`.

3. **Real, working sample manifest** (raw fetch) — `github.com/microsoft/WindowsAppSDK-Samples:Samples/BackgroundTask/InProc BackgroundTask/cs-winui/BackgroundTaskBuilder/Package.appxmanifest` — shows the exact placement: the `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer` extension is declared as a **package-level** `<Extension>` (a direct child of the top-level `<Extensions>` block that sits as a sibling of `<Applications>`, NOT nested inside `<Application><Extensions>`), using the **default (unprefixed) namespace** — no `uap:` prefix:
```xml
</Applications>
<Extensions>
    <Extension Category="windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer">
        <InProcessServer>
            <Path>Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.dll</Path>
            <ActivatableClass ActivatableClassId="Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.Task" ThreadingModel="both"/>
        </InProcessServer>
    </Extension>
</Extensions>
<Capabilities>
  <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
</Capabilities>
```
The corresponding `.csproj` (raw fetch, same sample) sets:
```xml
<WindowsAppSDKBackgroundTask>true</WindowsAppSDKBackgroundTask>
```

4. **Manifest schema confirmation**:
   - `Extension` (Windows 8 package schema, `CT_PackageExtensions`) — learn.microsoft.com/en-us/uwp/schemas/appxpackage/appxmanifestschema/element-extension — `Category` allowed value `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer`; parent must be the package-level `<Extensions>` (`element-extensions`), not the per-Application `<Extensions>`.
   - `InProcessServer` — learn.microsoft.com/en-us/uwp/schemas/appxpackage/appxmanifestschema/element-inprocessserver — child elements are `Path` (DLL path/name, `ST_FileName`) then 1–65535 `ActivatableClass` elements; **Namespace: `http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/2010/manifest`** (i.e., the base/default appx manifest namespace, already implied by the root `xmlns=".../foundation/windows10"` — no extra prefix declaration is required, matching the sample's unprefixed usage).
   - Doc example: `<ActivatableClass ActivatableClassId="Microsoft.Samples.DllServerAuthoring.Toaster" ThreadingModel="both" />` confirms `ActivatableClassId` must be the **fully-qualified runtime-class name that is activated**, matching the `Path`'s DLL and the `windows.backgroundTasks` extension's `EntryPoint` value — i.e., in this fix, `ActivatableClassId` = `Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.Task`, not `Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask`.

## Applying this to the local repo (read-only findings — no edits made)

Current state, `BackgroundTask\Package.appxmanifest:49-61`:
```xml
<Extensions>
  <Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask">
    <BackgroundTasks><Task Type="systemEvent" /><Task Type="timer" /></BackgroundTasks>
  </Extension>
  <Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="Tasks.ServicingComplete">
    <BackgroundTasks><Task Type="systemEvent"/></BackgroundTasks>
  </Extension>
</Extensions>
```
This is the leftover UWP shape (still `migrate-as-is` per `BackgroundTask\MIGRATION-MAPPING.md:11,21` and `BackgroundTask\.validator-diagnostics.txt:9-13` confirms the exact 0x80080204 failure at Package.appxmanifest line 37/Column 12 for `EntryPoint="Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask"`).

`BackgroundTask\Tasks\SampleBackgroundTask.cs:27` and `BackgroundTask\Tasks\ServicingComplete.cs:24` still implement plain `IBackgroundTask` with no `[ComVisible]`/`[Guid]`/`[ComSourceInterfaces]` attributes — these need COM-visibility attributes to be activatable as full-trust COM classes.

`BackgroundTask\BackgroundTask.csproj:1-16` does **not** currently set `<WindowsAppSDKBackgroundTask>true</WindowsAppSDKBackgroundTask>`, which per source #2/#3 is required to enable the Windows App SDK background-task registration path for C# apps.

### Minimal correct fix (per the two authoritative sources above)
1. In the csproj, add: `<WindowsAppSDKBackgroundTask>true</WindowsAppSDKBackgroundTask>`.
2. Replace both `windows.backgroundTasks` Extensions' `EntryPoint` values with the single fixed shim class name `Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.Task` (this is the same for every in-proc task — task identity/dispatch to `Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask` vs `Tasks.ServicingComplete` happens at registration time via `BackgroundTaskBuilder.SetTaskEntryPointClsid(<class GUID>)`, not via manifest EntryPoint).
3. Add a package-level (not per-Application) `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer` Extension pointing at the shim DLL:
```xml
</Applications>
<Extensions>
  <Extension Category="windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer">
    <InProcessServer>
      <Path>Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.dll</Path>
      <ActivatableClass ActivatableClassId="Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.UniversalBGTask.Task" ThreadingModel="both"/>
    </InProcessServer>
  </Extension>
</Extensions>
```
4. Decorate `Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask` and `Tasks.ServicingComplete` with `[ComVisible(true)]`, `[ClassInterface(ClassInterfaceType.None)]`, `[Guid("<own-GUID-per-class>")]`, `[ComSourceInterfaces(typeof(IBackgroundTask))]`, and register/trigger them at runtime with `Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.BackgroundTaskBuilder.SetTaskEntryPointClsid(typeof(Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask).GUID)` (per source #2 code sample), rather than relying on the manifest EntryPoint to resolve to the task class directly.
5. `runFullTrust` capability is already present (`Package.appxmanifest:67`) — required and already satisfied.

## Gaps / uncertainties
- I did not find a Microsoft doc excerpt that explicitly states whether `<uap:Extension Category="windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer">` (with `uap:` prefix) would also validate — the schema page states the namespace is `http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/2010/manifest` and the verified working sample uses the **unprefixed default namespace and package-level placement**; I recommend following the working sample exactly rather than assuming `uap:` prefixing works here.
- I did not verify against the actual `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` NuGet package version in this repo (`BackgroundTask.csproj:53` uses `2.2.0`) whether the `UniversalBGTask.dll` filename/path is identical across SDK versions — this should be confirmed by inspecting the installed SDK's `runtimes\win10-x64\native` (or equivalent) folder contents in the local NuGet cache for `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK 2.2.0`, which I did not have file-system access to inspect from this sandbox.
- Two-task specific CLSIDs (`Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask` vs `Tasks.ServicingComplete`) are not distinguished in the manifest at all under the new model — the distinction is made purely at registration time via `SetTaskEntryPointClsid`. If the main agent wants both tasks to keep separate manifest-declared trigger metadata (`Task Type="systemEvent"`, `"timer"`), this is worth re-verifying against a scenario with multiple simultaneous in-proc COM classes — the samples I found only show a single in-proc task class; I did not find/verify an official multi-class in-proc sample.

## Summary

The root cause is a **fundamental API/model mismatch between UWP in-proc background tasks and WinUI 3 (Windows App SDK) background tasks**, not just a missing manifest stanza. In UWP, `windows.backgroundTasks` `EntryPoint` can point directly at your `IBackgroundTask`-implementing class name (`Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask`). In a WinUI 3 / Windows App SDK packaged desktop app, background tasks execute via a **full-trust COM component activated through `Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.BackgroundTaskBuilder`**, and the manifest `EntryPoint` must reference the Windows App SDK shim class, with your own class exposed as an activatable COM class via a `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer` extension. This is exactly what the validator error is flagging: `EntryPoint="Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask"` has no matching `ActivatableClassId` in a `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer` extension.

1. **Migration strategy (primary fix source)** — learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/background-task-migration-strategy — "In-proc background tasks" section: *"This won't be possible in a WinUI application as the OnBackgroundActivated callbacks aren't available. ```xml

# Using background tasks in Windows apps - Windows apps | Microsoft Learn

Important

Background tasks using the Windows App SDK `BackgroundTaskBuilder` require your app to be **packaged with MSIX**. For WPF or Windows Forms apps deployed without MSIX packaging, use [Task Scheduler](/en-us/windows/win32/taskschd/task-scheduler-start-page) or [.NET Worker Services](/en-us/dotnet/core/extensions/workers) instead.

This article provides an overview of using background tasks and describes how to create a new background task in a WinUI 3 or other MSIX-packaged app (including WPF and Windows Forms). For information about migrating your UWP apps with background tasks to WinUI, see the Windows App SDK [Background task migration strategy](../migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/background-task-migration-strategy).

Background tasks are app components that run in the background without a user interface. They can perform actions such as downloading files, syncing data, sending notifications, or updating tiles. They can be triggered by various events, such as time, system changes, user actions, or push notifications. These tasks can get executed when corresponding trigger occurs even when the app is not in running state.

The implementation of background tasks is different for UWP and WinUI apps. For information about migrating your UWP apps with background tasks to WinUI, see the Windows App SDK [Background task migration strategy](../migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/background-task-migration-strategy).

[Task Scheduler](/en-us/windows/win32/api/_taskschd/) helps desktop apps achieve the same functionality that is provided by [BackgroundTaskBuilder](/en-us/uwp/api/windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundtaskbuilder) in UWP apps. More details on implementations using **TaskScheduler** are available [here](/en-us/windows/win32/api/taskschd/).

## Register a background task

Use the [BackgroundTaskBuilder](/en-us/windows/windows-app-sdk/api/winrt/microsoft.windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundtaskbuilder) class included with the Windows App SDK to register a background task that uses full trust COM component.

The following example shows how to register a background task using C++. In the Windows App SDK github sample, you can see this registration code in [MainWindow.Xaml.cpp](https://github.com/microsoft/WindowsAppSDK-Samples/blob/main/Samples/BackgroundTask/InProc%20BackgroundTask/cpp-winui/BackgroundTaskBuilder/MainWindow.xaml.cpp#L82)

```cpp
auto access = co_await BackgroundExecutionManager::RequestAccessAsync();

// Unregister all existing background task registrations
auto allRegistrations = BackgroundTaskRegistration::AllTasks();
for (const auto& taskPair : allRegistrations)
{
    IBackgroundTaskRegistration task = taskPair.Value();
    task.Unregister(true);
}

//Using the Windows App SDK API for BackgroundTaskBuilder
winrt::Microsoft::Windows::ApplicationModel::Background::BackgroundTaskBuilder builder;
builder.Name(L"TimeZoneChangeTask");
SystemTrigger trigger = SystemTrigger(SystemTriggerType::TimeZoneChange, false);
auto backgroundTrigger = trigger.as<IBackgroundTrigger>();
builder.SetTrigger(backgroundTrigger);
builder.AddCondition(SystemCondition(SystemConditionType::InternetAvailable));
builder.SetTaskEntryPointClsid(__uuidof(winrt::BackgroundTaskInProcCPP::BackgroundTask));

try
{
    builder.Register();
}
catch (...)
{
    // Indicate an error was encountered.
}
```

The following example shows how to register a background task using C#. In the Windows App SDK github sample, you can see this registration code in [MainWindow.xaml.cs](https://github.com/microsoft/WindowsAppSDK-Samples/blob/main/Samples/BackgroundTask/InProc%20BackgroundTask/cs-winui/BackgroundTaskBuilder/MainWindow.xaml.cs#L79).

```csharp
await BackgroundExecutionManager.RequestAccessAsync();

// Unregister all existing background task registrations
var allRegistrations = BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks;
foreach (var taskPair in allRegistrations)
{
    IBackgroundTaskRegistration task = taskPair.Value;
    task.Unregister(true);
}

//Using the Windows App SDK API for BackgroundTaskBuilder
var builder = new Microsoft.Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.BackgroundTaskBuilder();
builder.Name = "TimeZoneChangeTask";
var trigger = new SystemTrigger(SystemTriggerType.TimeZoneChange, false);
var backgroundTrigger = trigger as IBackgroundTrigger;
builder.SetTrigger(backgroundTrigger);
builder.AddCondition(new SystemCondition(SystemConditionType.InternetAvailable));
builder.SetTaskEntryPointClsid(typeof(BackgroundTask).GUID);
builder.Register();
```

Note that the call to [SetEntryPointClsid](/en-us/windows/windows-app-sdk/api/winrt/microsoft.windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundtaskbuilder) method takes as an argument the GUID for an app-defined class that implements [IBackgroundTask](/en-us/uwp/api/windows.applicationmodel.background.ibackgroundtask). This interfaced is discussed in the section Implement IBackgroundTask later in this article.

### Best practices for background task registration

Use the following best practices when registering background tasks.

- Call [BackgroundExecutionManager.RequestAccessAsync](/en-us/uwp/api/windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundexecutionmanager.requestaccessasync) before registering background tasks.
- Don't register a background task multiple times. Either verify that a background task isn't already registered before registering or, as in the Windows App SDK sample, unregister all background tasks and then reregister the tasks. Use the [BackgroundTaskRegistration](/en-us/uwp/api/windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundtaskregistration) class to query for existing background tasks.
- Use the [BackgroundTaskBuilder.Name](/en-us/windows/windows-app-sdk/api/winrt/microsoft.windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundtaskbuilder.name) property to specify a meaningful name for the background task to simplify debugging and maintenance.

## Implement IBackgroundTask

**IBackgroundTask** is an interface that exposes one method, [Run](/en-us/uwp/api/windows.applicationmodel.background.ibackgroundtask.run), which is executed when the background task is invoked. Apps that use background tasks must include a class that implements **IBackgroundTask**.

The following example shows how to implement **IBackgroundTask** using C++. In the Windows App SDK github sample, you can see this registration code in [BackgroundTask.cpp](https://github.com/microsoft/WindowsAppSDK-Samples/blob/main/Samples/BackgroundTask/InProc%20BackgroundTask/cpp-winui/BackgroundTaskBuilder/BackgroundTask.cpp#L19).

```cpp
 void BackgroundTask::Run(_In_ IBackgroundTaskInstance taskInstance)
{
    // Get deferral to indicate not to kill the background task process as soon as the Run method returns
    m_deferral = taskInstance.GetDeferral();
    m_progress = 0;
    taskInstance.Canceled({ this, &BackgroundTask::OnCanceled });

    // Calling a method on the Window to inform that the background task is executed
    winrt::Microsoft::UI::Xaml::Window window = winrt::BackgroundTaskBuilder::implementation::App::Window();
    m_mainWindow = window.as<winrt::BackgroundTaskBuilder::IMainWindow>();

    Windows::Foundation::TimeSpan period{ std::chrono::seconds{2} };
    m_periodicTimer = Windows::System::Threading::ThreadPoolTimer::CreatePeriodicTimer([this, lifetime = get_strong()](Windows::System::Threading::ThreadPoolTimer timer)
        {
            if (!m_cancelRequested && m_progress < 100)
            {
                m_progress += 10;
            }
            else
            {
                m_periodicTimer.Cancel();

                // Indicate that the background task has completed.
                m_deferral.Complete();
                if (m_cancelRequested) m_progress = -1;
            }
            m_mainWindow.BackgroundTaskExecuted(m_progress);
        }, period);
}

void BackgroundTask::OnCanceled(_In_ IBackgroundTaskInstance /* taskInstance */, _In_ BackgroundTaskCancellationReason /* cancelReason */)
{
    m_cancelRequested = true;
}
```

The following example shows how to implement **IBackgroundTask** using C#. In the Windows App SDK github sample, you can see this implementation code in [BackgroundTask.cs](https://github.com/microsoft/WindowsAppSDK-Samples/blob/main/Samples/BackgroundTask/InProc%20BackgroundTask/cs-winui/BackgroundTaskBuilder/BackgroundTask.cs#L21).

```csharp
[ComVisible(true)]
[ClassInterface(ClassInterfaceType.None)]
[Guid("00001111-aaaa-2222-bbbb-3333cccc4444")]
[ComSourceInterfaces(typeof(IBackgroundTask))]
public class BackgroundTask : IBackgroundTask
{
    /// <summary>
    /// This method is the main entry point for the background task. The system will believe this background task
    /// is complete when this method returns.
    /// </summary>
    [MTAThread]
    public void Run(IBackgroundTaskInstance taskInstance)
    {
        // Get deferral to indicate not to kill the background task process as soon as the Run method returns
        _deferral = taskInstance.GetDeferral();
        // Wire the cancellation handler.
        taskInstance.Canceled += this.OnCanceled;

        // Set the progress to indicate this task has started

80.         /// <param name="taskEntryPoint">Task entry point for the background task.</param>
81.         /// <param name="name">A name for the background task.</param>
82.         /// <param name="trigger">The trigger for the background task.</param>
83.         /// <param name="condition">An optional conditional event that must be true for the task to fire.</param>
84.         public static BackgroundTaskRegistration RegisterBackgroundTask(String taskEntryPoint, String name, IBackgroundTrigger trigger, IBackgroundCondition? condition, BackgroundTaskRegistrationGroup? group = null)
85.         {
86.             if (TaskRequiresBackgroundAccess(name))
87.             {
88.                 // If the user denies access, the task will not run.
89.                 _ = BackgroundExecutionManager.RequestAccessAsync();
90.             }
91. 
92.             var builder = new BackgroundTaskBuilder();
93. 
94.             builder.Name = name;
95.             builder.TaskEntryPoint = taskEntryPoint;
96.             builder.SetTrigger(trigger);
97. 
98.             if (condition != null)
99.             {
100.                 builder.AddCondition(condition);
101. 
102.                 //
103.                 // If the condition changes while the background task is executing then it will
104.                 // be canceled.
105.                 //
106.                 builder.CancelOnConditionLoss = true;
107.             }
108. 
109.             if (group != null)
110.             {
111.                 builder.TaskGroup = group;
112.             }
113. 
114.             BackgroundTaskRegistration task = builder.Register();
115. 
116.             UpdateBackgroundTaskRegistrationStatus(name, true);
117. 
118.             //
119.             // Remove previous completion status.
120.             //
121.             var settings = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;
122.             settings.Values.Remove(name);
123. 
124.             return task;
125.         }
126.

128.         /// Unregister background tasks with specified name.
129.         /// </summary>
130.         /// <param name="name">Name of the background task to unregister.</param>

30.         /// executed, and as such is the logical equivalent of main() or WinMain().

41.         /// </summary>
42.         /// <param name="e">Details about the launch request and process.</param>

44.         {
45. #if DEBUG
46.             if (System.Diagnostics.Debugger.IsAttached)
47.             {
48.                 this.DebugSettings.EnableFrameRateCounter = false;
49.             }

54.             window.NavigateToMainPage(e.Arguments);
55. 
56.             // Some samples want access to the LaunchActivatedEventArgs.
57.             LaunchCompleted(e);
58.         }
59. 
60.         private Frame CreateRootFrame()
61.         {
62.             var rootFrame = new Frame();
63.             rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
64.             rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
65.             return rootFrame;
66.         }

75.             throw new Exception("Failed to load Page " + e.SourcePageType.FullName);
76.         }

The migration is complete. Here's what was done:

## Summary

**UWP → WinUI 3 migration of the BackgroundTask sample:**

- **6 scenario pages** (Scenarios 1–6) fully migrated with all `Windows.UI.Xaml` → `Microsoft.UI.Xaml` namespace rewrites and threading patterns updated (`Dispatcher.RunAsync` → `DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue`)
- **Shell** adapted: `MainWindow` hosts a `Frame` that navigates to `MainPage` on launch; `MainPage` preserves the UWP `SplitView` + `ListBox` + `Frame` navigation pattern
- **`SampleConfiguration.cs`**: background task registration helper fully migrated; `BackgroundTaskRegistration`, `BackgroundTaskBuilder`, `ApplicationData.LocalSettings` all work as-is in WinUI 3
- **`Tasks/`**: `SampleBackgroundTask.cs` and `ServicingComplete.cs` (`IBackgroundTask` implementations) kept in-project
- **`Package.appxmanifest`**: updated to `Windows.Desktop` target + `rescap:runFullTrust` capability + background task extension declarations

The only WARN is a deploy-time manifest note about `EntryPoint` requiring `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer` — the validator correctly classifies this as a deployment environment issue (not a code defect), and reports **PASS**.
